It:\n- Calls `loadObjs` to load raw data files\n- Calls `processData` for each session/probe combination\n- Handles single vs. dual probe scenarios by concatenating data appropriately\n- Converts parameter structure from cell arrays to struct arrays\n\n---\n\n## 2. DATA LOADING - loadObjs.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/loadObjs.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction obj = loadObjs(meta)\nfor i = 1:numel(meta)\n    dat = load(meta(i).datapth);\n    if ~isfield(dat.obj,'meta')\n        dat.obj.meta = 'assigning to avoid dissimilar struct error';\n    end\n    if ~isfield(dat.obj,'ex')\n        dat.obj.ex = 'assigning to avoid dissimilar struct error';\n    end\n    if ~isfield(dat.obj,'me')\n        dat.obj.me = 'assigning to avoid dissimilar struct error';\n    end\n    obj(i) = dat.obj;\n    clear dat\nend\nend\n```\n\nSimple loader that:\n- Loads each .mat file specified in `meta.datapth`\n- Assigns placeholder fields if missing to avoid struct dissimilarity errors\n- Returns array of loaded data objects\n\n---\n\n## 3. TRIAL FILTERING - findTrials.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction trialNums = findTrials(obj, conditions)\n\nvarnames = getStructVarNames(obj);\nNtrials = obj.bp.Ntrials;\nfor i = 1:numel(varnames)\n    eval([varnames{i} ' = obj.bp.' varnames{i} ';']);\n    \n    if eval(['numel(' varnames{i} ')==obj.bp.Ntrials && isrow(' varnames{i} ')'])\n        eval([varnames{i} '=' varnames{i} ''';']);\n    end\n    \n    eval([varnames{i} '=' varnames{i} '(~isnan(' varnames{i} '))' ''';'])\nend\n\nif isfield (obj.bp, 'fidx')\n    obj.bp.Ntrials = sum(obj.bp.fidx==2);\n    addToTrialNums = sum(obj.bp.fidx==1);\nend\n\nmask = zeros(obj.bp.Ntrials, numel(conditions));\n\nfor i = 1:numel(conditions)\n    try\n        mask(:,i) = eval(conditions{i});\n    catch\n        mask(:,i) = eval(conditions{i}{1});\n    end\n    if exist('addToTrialNums','var') == 1\n        trialNums{i} = find(mask(:,i)) + addToTrialNums;\n    else\n        trialNums{i} = find(mask(:,i));\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nFilters trials by dynamically evaluating condition strings. The function:\n- Extracts all trial variables from `obj.bp` using `getStructVarNames`\n- Removes NaN values from each variable\n- Evaluates condition strings (e.g., `'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'`)\n- Handles older data with `fidx` field (first vs. second half of recording)\n- Returns cell array of trial indices for each condition\n\n**Typical conditions from getDefaultParams.m:**\n```matlab\nparams.condition(1) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};      % right hits, no stim, aw off\nparams.condition(2) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};      % left hits, no stim, aw off\nparams.condition(3) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};       % right hits, no stim, aw on\nparams.condition(4) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};       % left hits, no stim, aw on\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. CLUSTER FILTERING - findClusters.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction idx = findClusters(qualityList, qualities)\n% find idx where qualityList contains at least one of the patterns in\n% qualities\n\n% remove leading/trailing spaces\n% also check if each entry in qualityList is a string/char, if not, change it\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\n    if ~ischar(qualityList{i}) && ~isstring(qualityList{i})\n        qualityList{i} = '';\n    end\nend\nqualityList = strtrim(qualityList);\n\nif any(strcmpi(qualities,'all')) || strcmpi(qualities{1},'all')\n    idx = find(~ismember(qualityList,'garbage') & ~ismember(qualityList,'gabrga') & ~ismember(qualityList,'noisy') & ~ismember(qualityList,'real?'));\n    return\nend\n\n% handle unlabeled cluster qualities\nfor i = 1:numel(qualityList)\n    if isempty(qualityList{i})\n        qualityList(i) = {'nan'};\n    end\nend\n\n[~,mask] = patternMatchCellArray(qualityList, qualities, 'any');\n\nidx = find(mask);\nend\n```\n\nFilters clusters by quality label. When `qualities = {'all'}`:\n- **EXCLUDES:** 'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'\n- **INCLUDES:** all other labels and unlabeled clusters\n\nThis is called in processData as:\n```matlab\nparams.cluid{prbnum} = findClusters({obj.clu{prbnum}(:).quality}', params.quality);\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. SPIKE ALIGNMENT - alignSpikes.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction obj = alignSpikes(obj,params,prbnum)\n\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'moveOnset')\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = findMoveOnset(obj);\nend\n\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'firstLick')\n    % get first lick time for left and right licks\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickL;\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\n    outL = zeros(size(temp));\n    outL(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(1),temp(idx));\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickR;\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\n    outR = zeros(size(temp));\n    outR(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(1),temp(idx));\n    firstLick = zeros(size(temp));\n    % firstLick = min(outL,outR), except when outL||outR == 0\n    outL(outL==0) = nan;\n    outR(outR==0) = nan;\n    firstLick = nanmin(outL,outR);\n    firstLick(isnan(firstLick)) = 0;\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = firstLick;\nend\n\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'lastLick')\n    % get last lick time for left and right licks\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickL;\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\n    outL = zeros(size(temp));\n    outL(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(end),temp(idx));\n    temp = obj.bp.ev.lickR;\n    idx = ~cellfun('isempty',temp);\n    outR = zeros(size(temp));\n    outR(idx) = cellfun(@(v)v(end),temp(idx));\n    lastLick = zeros(size(temp));\n    % lastLick = max(outL,outR), except when outL||outR == 0\n    outL(outL==0) = nan;\n    outR(outR==0) = nan;\n    lastLick = nanmax(outL,outR);\n    lastLick(isnan(lastLick)) = 0;\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = lastLick;\nend\n\nif strcmp(params.alignEvent,'jawOnset')\n    % half rise time to first peak of jaw onset after go cue\n    view = 1; % side cam\n    [~,jawMask] = patternMatchCellArray(obj.traj{view}(1).featNames,{'jaw'},'all');\n    feat = find(jawMask); % jaw\n    \n    % filter params\n    opts.f_cut = 60; % cutoff freq for butter filt\n    opts.f_n   = 2;  % filter order\n    \n    % peak finding params\n    opts.minpkdist = 0.06; % number of ms around peaks to reject peaks\n    opts.minpkprom = 10;   % a threshold for the peak size\n    obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent) = alignJawOnset(view,feat,obj,opts);\nend\n\n% align spikes to params.alignEvent\nfor clu = 1:numel(obj.clu{prbnum})\n    event = obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent)(obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trial);\n    obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trialtm_aligned = obj.clu{prbnum}(clu).trialtm - event;\nend\nend\n```\n\nAligns spike times to specified event. Default is 'goCue' but supports:\n- `'goCue'` - trial start signal\n- `'moveOnset'` - computed movement onset\n- `'firstLick'` - earliest lick (L or R)\n- `'lastLick'` - latest lick (L or R)\n- `'jawOnset'` - jaw onset based on video\n\nFor each cluster, subtracts the event time from each spike's trial time, storing result in `trialtm_aligned`.\n\n---\n\n## 6. SPIKE BINNING & PSTH - getSeq.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction obj = getSeq(obj,params,prbnum,varargin)\n\nif isfield(obj,'psth')\n    obj = rmfield(obj,{'psth','trialdat'});\nend\n\nif nargin > 3\n    doPSTH = varargin{1};\nelse\n    doPSTH = true;\nend\n\nedges = params.tmin:params.dt:params.tmax;\nobj.time = edges + params.dt/2;\nobj.time = obj.time(1:end-1);\n\nspkedges = params.tmin:0.001:params.tmax;\n\n% get psths by condition\nobj.psth{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),numel(params.condition));\nif doPSTH\n    for i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\n        curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\n        for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\n\n            trix = params.trialid{j};\n\n            spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\n\n            % if no spikes found for current set of trials (trix), move on to next set\n            if all(~spkix)\n                continue\n            end\n\n            N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\n            N = N(1:end-1);\n\n            obj.psth{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth, params.bctype);\n        end\n    end\nend\n\n% get single trial data\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),obj.bp.Ntrials);\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\n    curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\n    for j = 1:obj.bp.Ntrials\n\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, j);\n\n        % if no spikes found for current trial (j), move on to next trial\n        if all(~spkix)\n            continue\n        end\n\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm_aligned(spkix), edges);\n        N = N(1:end-1);\n        if size(N,2) > size(N,1)\n            N = N'; % make sure N is a column vector\n        end\n\n        obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./params.dt,params.smooth, params.bctype);\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nKey parameters from default settings:\n```matlab\nparams.tmin = -2.5;              % start time (s), relative to alignment event\nparams.tmax = 2.5;               % end time (s), relative to alignment event\nparams.dt = 1/200;               % bin size = 5 ms\nparams.smooth = 15;              % gaussian kernel window for smoothing\nparams.bctype = 'none';          % boundary condition type for smoothing\n```\n\nBins and smooths neural data:\n- Creates time bins: `edges = -2.5:0.005:2.5`\n- Counts spikes per bin using `histc`\n- Converts to firing rate: `count / (num_trials * dt)` for PSTHs\n- Normalizes by dt for single trials: `count / dt`\n- Applies causal Gaussian smoothing with kernel window N=15\n- Returns arrays: `psth(time x neuron x condition)` and `trialdat(time x neuron x trial)`\n\n---\n\n## 7. LOW FIRING RATE REMOVAL - removeLowFRClusters.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [obj,cluid] = removeLowFRClusters(obj,cluid,lowFR,prbnum)\n% Remove low-firing rate units, e.g., all those firing less than lowFR\n% spikes per second on average across all trials.\n%\n% The fitted observation noise (diagonal element of R) for a\n% low-firing rate unit will be small, so the neural trajectory may\n% show a deflection each time the neuron spikes.\n\nmeanFRs = mean(mean(obj.psth{prbnum},3,'omitnan'),'omitnan');\nuse = meanFRs > lowFR;\n\n% remove low fr clusters\ncluid = cluid(use);\nobj.psth{prbnum} = obj.psth{prbnum}(:,use,:);\nobj.trialdat{prbnum} = obj.trialdat{prbnum}(:,use,:);\nend\n```\n\nDefault threshold from getDefaultParams:\n```matlab\nparams.lowFR = 0.5;  % remove clusters with firing rates across all trials less than this val (spikes/sec)\n```\n\nRemoves neurons with mean firing rate < 0.5 Hz across all trials by:\n- Computing mean FR: average across time and conditions\n- Identifying low-FR neurons: `meanFRs > lowFR`\n- Removing them from cluster list and data arrays\n\n---\n\n## 8. MOTION ENERGY LOADING - loadMotionEnergy.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction me = loadMotionEnergy(obj,meta,params,datapth,varargin)\n\nif nargin > 4\n    returnAfterLoad = varargin{1};\nelse\n    returnAfterLoad = 0;\nend\n\n% find and load motion energy .mat file\nmepth = fullfile(datapth,'DataObjects',meta.anm);\ncontents = dir(mepth);\ncontents = contents(~[contents.isdir]);\n\nfns = {contents.name}';\n\nfn = patternMatchCellArray({contents.name}',{'motionEnergy',meta.date},'all');\n\nif numel(fn) == 1\n    fn = fn{1};\nelseif numel(fn) == 2\n    ix = ~contains(fn,'._'); % on windows, sometimes a file that starts with '._motionEnergy*' is found\n    fn = fn{ix};\nelse\n    disp('UNABLE TO LOCATE A MOTION ENERGY FILE IN: ')\n    disp(mepth)\n    disp('      Continuing without motion energy as a feature')\n    me.use = 0;\n    me.data = nan;\n    me.moveThresh = nan;\n    return\nend\n\ntemp = load(fullfile(mepth,fn));\nme = temp.me;\n\nif isstruct(me.data)\n    me.data = me.data.data;\nend\n\nif returnAfterLoad\n    return\nend\n\nvidshift = findVideoOffset(obj);\n% vidshift = 1;\n\n% -----------------------------------------------------------\n% trim trial length (me.data contains motion energy for each time point in\n% trial at 400 Hz). Want to align to params.alignEvent and want to put it\n% in same dt as neural data\n% -----------------------------------------------------------\ntaxis = obj.time + params.advance_movement;\nalignTimes = obj.bp.ev.(params.alignEvent);\nme.newdata = zeros(numel(obj.time),numel(me.data));\nfor trix = 1:numel(me.data)\n    try\n        me.newdata(:,trix) = interp1(obj.traj{1}(trix).frameTimes-vidshift-alignTimes(trix),me.data{trix},taxis);\n    catch % if frameTimes doesn't exist or is full of NaNs\n        frameTimes = (1:size(obj.traj{1}(trix).ts,1)) ./ 400;\n        me.newdata(:,trix) = interp1(frameTimes-0.5-alignTimes(trix),me.data{trix},taxis);\n    end\nend\n\n% replace me.data with me.newdata\nme.data = me.newdata;\nme = rmfield(me,'newdata');\n% fill nans with nearest value (there are some nans at the start of each trial)\nme.data = fillmissing(me.data,'nearest');\n\n% -------------------------------------------------------------------\n% -- assign move time points as logical array same size as me.data --\n% -------------------------------------------------------------------\nme.move = me.data > (me.moveThresh);\nend\n```\n\nLoads and processes motion energy from video:\n- Searches for `.mat` file matching animal name and session date\n- Interpolates from native 400 Hz video rate to neural data sampling rate (200 Hz)\n- Aligns to the trial alignment event using `findVideoOffset`\n- Creates logical `me.move` array where `me.data > me.moveThresh`\n- Fills NaNs at trial start with nearest value\n\n---\n\n## 9. VIDEO OFFSET CALCULATION - findVideoOffset.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/findVideoOffset.m`\n\n```matlab\n% find offset between neural data file start and video file start\nfunction vidshift = findVideoOffset(obj)\nbitStart = mode(obj.bp.ev.bitStart);\nvidFileOffset = mode(obj.sglx.bitcode.bitstart) / obj.sglx.fs;\nvidshift = vidFileOffset - bitStart; % (s)\nend\n```\n\nComputes video-to-neural alignment offset by:\n- Getting most common `bitStart` time from behavior events\n- Getting most common video bitcode start time, normalized by sampling frequency\n- Computing offset in seconds\n\n---\n\n## 10. KINEMATICS COMPUTATION - getKinematics.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematics.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction kin = getKinematics(obj,me,params)\n\n% Check if obj already has kin\nif isfield(obj,'kin')\n    if isfield(params,'reload_kin')\n        if params.reload_kin\n            % get kinematics\n        else\n            return % kin = obj.kin\n        end\n    end\n    return % kin = obj.kin\nelse\n    % get kinematics\nend\n\n% Kinematics\n% (xdisp,ydisp,xvel,yvel) for each dlc feature\n[kin.dat,kin.featLeg,kin.nans] = getKinematicsFromVideo(obj,params);\n\n% Tongue angle and length\nif ismember('tongue',params.traj_features{1})\n    [ang,len] = getLickAngleAndLength(kin.featLeg,kin.dat,kin.nans);\n    kin.featLeg{end+1} = 'tongue_angle';\n    kin.featLeg{end+1} = 'tongue_length';\n    \n    kin.dat(:,:,end+1) = ang;\n    kin.dat(:,:,end+1) = len;\nend\n\n% Motion energy\nkin.featLeg{end+1} = 'motion_energy';\nkin.dat(:,:,end+1) = me.data;\n\n% Standardize kinematics\nfor featix = 1:size(kin.dat,3)\n    temp = kin.dat(:,:,featix);\n    temp2 = temp(:);\n    kin.dat_std(:,:,featix) = (temp - mean(temp2,'omitnan')) ./ std(temp2,'omitnan'); \nend\n\n% DIMENSIONALITY REDUCTION\n% Many video features will be highly correlated, so we perform PCA/FA\n% to reduce dimensionality to a set of factors that best explain the movement\nkin.dat_reduced = reduceDimensionVideoFeatures(kin.dat,params.feat_varToExplain);\nend\n```\n\nComprehensive kinematics processing pipeline:\n1. Extracts position and velocity for each DLC feature from video\n2. Computes tongue angle and length from multi-point tracking\n3. Includes motion energy from video\n4. Standardizes all features to z-score\n5. Applies PCA to reduce dimensionality\n\nDefault video features from getDefaultParams.m:\n```matlab\nparams.traj_features = {{'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},...\n                        {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','jaw','top_paw','bottom_paw','top_nostril','bottom_nostril'}};\nparams.feat_varToExplain = 80; % PCA components should explain 80% variance\nparams.advance_movement = 0.0;  % time offset for movement relative to neural data (s)\n```\n\n---\n\n### 10a. getKinematicsFromVideo.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [kin,featLeg,nans] = getKinematicsFromVideo(obj,params)\n\n% Parameters\nNvarsPerFeat = 4; % (xdisp,ydisp,xvel,yvel)\nfeats = params.traj_features; % features to extract kinematics for\ntaxis = obj.time + params.advance_movement; % time axis to align to\nnTrials = obj.bp.Ntrials;\n\n% Initialize position and velocity arrays\nxpos = cell(2,1); % one entry for each view\nypos = cell(2,1);\nxvel = cell(2,1);\nyvel = cell(2,1);\n\nfor viewix = 1:numel(feats) % loop through cam0 and cam1\n    xpos{viewix} = nan(numel(obj.time),nTrials);\n    ypos{viewix} = nan(numel(obj.time),nTrials);\n    xvel{viewix} = nan(numel(obj.time),nTrials);\n    yvel{viewix} = nan(numel(obj.time),nTrials);\n    for featix = 1:numel(feats{viewix}) % loop through features for current cam\n        feat = feats{viewix}{featix};\n\n        [xpos{viewix}(:,:,featix), ypos{viewix}(:,:,featix)] = findPosition(taxis, obj, nTrials, viewix, feat, params.alignEvent);\n        [xvel{viewix}(:,:,featix), yvel{viewix}(:,:,featix)] = findVelocity(xpos{viewix}(:,:,featix), ypos{viewix}(:,:,featix), feat);\n    end\nend\n\n[xpos, ypos, nans] = setTongueBaselinePosition(xpos,ypos,feats,nTrials);\n\n% Compute displacement and velocity matrix\nfeatMat = cell(2,1);\nviewNum = cell(2,1);\nfor viewix = 1:numel(feats) % loop through cam0 and cam1\n    featMat{viewix} = nan(size(xpos{viewix}, 1), size(xpos{viewix}, 2), size(xpos{viewix}, 3).*NvarsPerFeat);\n    for featix = 1:numel(feats{viewix}) % loop through features for current cam\n        tempx = xpos{viewix}(:,:,featix);\n        tempy = ypos{viewix}(:,:,featix);\n\n        % Feature matrix organization: [xdisp, ydisp, xvel, yvel] for each feature\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,(featix*NvarsPerFeat)-3) = tempx;\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,featix*NvarsPerFeat-2) = tempy;\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,featix*NvarsPerFeat-1) = xvel{viewix}(:, :, featix);\n        featMat{viewix}(:,:,featix*NvarsPerFeat) = yvel{viewix}(:, :, featix);\n        viewNum{viewix}(featix) = viewix;\n    end\nend\n\n% Concatenate features into one big matrix of size (time, features*4, trials)\nkin = cat(3, featMat{:});\nv = cat(2, viewNum{:});\n\nallfeats = cat(2, feats{:});\nfeatLeg = cell(size(kin, 3), 1);\nfor i = 1:numel(allfeats)\n    featLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat-3} = [allfeats{i} '_xdisp_view' num2str(v(i))];\n    featLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat-2} = [allfeats{i} '_ydisp_view' num2str(v(i))];\n    featLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat-1} = [allfeats{i} '_xvel_view' num2str(v(i))];\n    featLeg{i*NvarsPerFeat} = [allfeats{i} '_yvel_view' num2str(v(i))];\nend\n\nend\n```\n\nReturns `kin` with shape `(time x trials x features*4)` containing all displacement and velocity components.\n\n---\n\n### 10b. findVelocity.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [xvel, yvel] = findVelocity(xpos, ypos, feat)\n\nfor i = 1:size(xpos,2) % for each trial\n    tsinterp = [xpos(:,i) ypos(:,i)];\n    basederiv = median(diff(tsinterp),'omitnan');\n\n    % find the difference between the feat velocity and the baseline feature velocity\n    xvel(:,i) = gradient(tsinterp(:,1));\n    yvel(:,i) = gradient(tsinterp(:,2));\n    \n    % For non-tongue features, subtract baseline drift\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xvel(:,i) = xvel(:,i) - basederiv(1);\n        yvel(:,i) = yvel(:,i) - basederiv(1);\n    end\n\n    % Fill missing values for all features\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xvel(:,i) = fillmissing(xvel(:,i),'nearest');\n        yvel(:,i) = fillmissing(yvel(:,i),'nearest');\n    else\n        tempx = xvel(:,i);\n        tempx(isnan(tempx)) = 0; % set tongue velocity to 0 if not visible\n        xvel(:,i) = tempx;\n        tempy = yvel(:,i);\n        tempy(isnan(tempy)) = 0; % set tongue velocity to 0 if not visible\n        yvel(:,i) = tempy;\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nComputes velocity using discrete gradient. For non-tongue features, subtracts baseline drift.\n\n---\n\n### 10c. findPosition.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/findPosition.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [xpos, ypos] = findPosition(taxis, obj, nTrials, view, feat, alignEv)\n\ntraj = obj.traj{view};\nfeatix = findDLCFeatIndex(obj.traj,view,{feat});\n\nxpos = nan(numel(taxis),nTrials);\nypos = nan(numel(taxis),nTrials);\n\nvidshift = findVideoOffset(obj);\n\nfor i = 1:nTrials % for each trial\n    trix = i;\n    \n    % check if video data for trial is good, skip if not\n    if isfield(traj(trix),'NdroppedFrames')\n        if isnan(traj(trix).NdroppedFrames )\n            continue;\n        end\n    end\n    \n    % check if frameTimes exists, if not, create it\n    if ~isfield(traj(trix),'frameTimes')\n        traj(trix).frameTimes = (1:size(traj(trix).ts,1)) ./ 400;\n    end\n    \n    % get feat trajectories for valid trials\n    if ~isnan(traj(trix).frameTimes)\n        ts = traj(trix).ts(:,1:2,featix);\n        % if tongue, don't smooth\n        if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n            ts = mySmooth(ts, 1, 'reflect');\n        end\n        % linear interpolation of frameTimes to taxis\n        tsinterp = interp1(traj(trix).frameTimes-vidshift-obj.bp.ev.(alignEv)(trix), ts, taxis);\n    end\n    \n    xpos(:,i) = tsinterp(:,1);\n    ypos(:,i) = tsinterp(:,2);\n    \n    % fill missing values for all features except tongue\n    if ~contains(feat,'tongue')\n        xpos(:,i) = fillmissing(xpos(:,i),'nearest');\n        ypos(:,i) = fillmissing(ypos(:,i),'nearest');\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nExtracts DLC positions for a given feature across trials:\n- Skips trials with dropped frames\n- Interpolates from 400 Hz video rate to neural time axis\n- Aligns to trial event using video offset\n- Applies light smoothing (window=1) to non-tongue features only\n\n---\n\n### 10d. getLickAngleAndLength.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/getLickAngleAndLength.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [ang,len] = getLickAngleAndLength(featLeg,kin,nans)\n\n% Extract tongue coordinates from view 2 (bottom camera)\n[~,ix1] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'top_tongue_xdisp_view2'},'all');\nt1x = kin(:, :, ix1);\nt1x = setNans(t1x,'top_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix2] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'topleft_tongue_xdisp_view2'},'all');\nt2x = kin(:, :, ix2);\nt2x = setNans(t2x,'topleft_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix1] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'top_tongue_ydisp_view2'},'all');\nt1y = kin(:, :, ix1);\nt1y = setNans(t1y,'top_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix2] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'topleft_tongue_ydisp_view2'},'all');\nt2y = kin(:, :, ix2);\nt2y = setNans(t2y,'topleft_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix1] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'bottom_tongue_xdisp_view2'},'all');\nt3x = kin(:, :, ix1);\nt3x = setNans(t3x,'bottom_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix2] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'bottomleft_tongue_xdisp_view2'},'all');\nt4x = kin(:, :, ix2);\nt4x = setNans(t4x,'bottomleft_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix1] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'bottom_tongue_ydisp_view2'},'all');\nt3y = kin(:, :, ix1);\nt3y = setNans(t3y,'bottom_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix2] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'bottomleft_tongue_ydisp_view2'},'all');\nt4y = kin(:, :, ix2);\nt4y = setNans(t4y,'bottomleft_tongue',nans);\n\n[~,ix1] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'jaw_xdisp_view2'},'all');\n[~,ix2] = patternMatchCellArray(featLeg,{'jaw_ydisp_view2'},'all');\njx = kin(:, :, ix1);\njy = kin(:, :, ix2);\n\n% Compute tongue tip and base positions as averages of tracked points\ntongue_tip_x = 0.5.*(t1x+t3x);   % average of top and bottom tongue points\ntongue_tip_y = 0.5.*(t1y+t3y);\n\ntongue_base_x = 0.5.*(t2x+t4x);  % average of topleft and bottomleft tongue points\ntongue_base_y = 0.5.*(t2y+t4y);\n\n% Compute angle and length relative to jaw\ndx = (tongue_tip_x-jx);\ndy = (tongue_tip_y-jy);\nang = atan(dy./dx);\nang(dx<0 & dy>0) = ang(dx<0 & dy>0) + pi;     % Quadrant correction\nang(dx<0 & dy<0) = ang(dx<0 & dy<0) - pi;\nlen = sqrt(dx.^2 + dy.^2);\nend\n```\n\nComputes tongue angle and length (extension) relative to jaw position.\n\n---\n\n### 10e. reduceDimensionVideoFeatures.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/funcs/kinematics/reduceDimensionVideoFeatures.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction feats_reduced = reduceDimensionVideoFeatures(feats,varToExplain)\n\nfeats_rsz = reshape(feats, size(feats, 1)*size(feats, 2), size(feats, 3));   % (time*trials x feats)\n\n% how many PCs needed to explain target variance\nfeats_rsz = fillmissing(feats_rsz,'nearest');\nfeats_rsz(isnan(feats_rsz)) = 0;\n[~, ~, ~, ~, explained] = pca(feats_rsz);\nnumFactors = numComponentsToExplainVariance(explained, varToExplain);\n\n[~,feats_reduced]  = pca(feats_rsz,'NumComponents',numFactors);\nfeats_reduced = reshape(feats_reduced,size(feats,1),size(feats,2),size(feats_reduced,2));\nend\n```\n\nReduces video features via PCA to explain 80% variance (default).\n\n---\n\n## 11. SMOOTHING - mySmooth.m\n\n**File:** `/app/code/utils/mySmooth.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [out,kernsd] = mySmooth(x, N, varargin)\n% Gaussian kernel smoothing operating on first dimension only\n% varargin options:\n%   'reflect' - reflect N elements from beginning of time series across 0 index\n%   'zeropad' - pad N zeros across 0 index\n%   'none'    - don't handle boundary conditions\n\n% returns:\n% - out: filtered data, same size as x\n% - kernsd: std dev of gaussian kernel\n\nif N == 0 || N == 1 % no smoothing\n    out = x;\n    kernsd = 0;\n    return\nend\n\nif isrow(x) % if 1-d time series, ensure column vector\n    x = x';\nend\n\nif nargin > 2\n    % handle a boundary condition\n    bctype = varargin{1};\n    if strcmpi(bctype,'reflect')\n        x_filt = cat(1,x(1:N,:),x);\n        trim = N + 1;\n    elseif strcmpi(bctype,'zeropad')\n        x_filt = cat(1,zeros(N,size(x,2)),x);\n        trim = N + 1;\n    elseif strcmpi(bctype,'none')\n        x_filt = x;\n        trim = 1;\n    else\n        warning('invalid boundary condition type - regular smoothing');\n        x_filt = x;\n        trim = 1;\n    end\nelse\n    % no bc handling\n    x_filt = x;\n    trim = 1;\nend\n\nNcol = size(x_filt, 2);\nNel = size(x_filt, 1);\n\nkern = gausswin(N);\nkernsd = std(1:N);\n\nkern(1:floor(numel(kern)/2)) = 0; % CAUSAL kernel (only past samples)\nkern = kern./sum(kern);\n\nout = zeros(Nel, Ncol);\nfor j = 1:Ncol\n    out(:, j) = conv(x_filt(:, j), kern, 'same');\nend\nout = out(trim:end,:);\nend\n```\n\n**Key feature: CAUSAL smoothing** - Only uses past samples (first half of kernel set to 0)\n\nDefault parameters from getDefaultParams.m:\n```matlab\nparams.smooth = 15;    % gaussian kernel window size\nparams.bctype = 'none'; % boundary condition type\n```\n\n---\n\n## 12. RECORDING & VIDEO CONFIGURATION\n\nExample session loading scripts in `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/`:\n\n### loadEKH1_ALMVideo.m\n```matlab\nmeta(end+1).anm = 'EKH1';\nmeta(end).datapth = datapth;\nmeta(end).date = '2021-08-07';\nmeta(end).probe = 2;\n```\nShows that EKH1 used **Probe 2** on 2021-08-07.\n\n### loadJEB4_ALMVideo.m\n```matlab\nmeta(end+1).anm = 'JEB4';\nmeta(end).date = '2021-03-14';\nmeta(end).probe = 1;\n\nmeta(end+1) = meta(end);\nmeta(end).date = '2021-03-15';\nmeta(end).probe = 2;\n\nmeta(end+1) = meta(end);\nmeta(end).date = '2021-03-16';\nmeta(end).probe = 1;\n```\nShows that JEB4 had:\n- 2021-03-14: **Probe 1**\n- 2021-03-15: **Probe 2**\n- 2021-03-16: **Probe 1**\n\n### loadJGR2_ALMVideo.m\n```matlab\nmeta(end+1).anm = 'JGR2';\nmeta(end).date = '2021-11-16';\nmeta(end).probe = 1;\n\nmeta(end+1) = meta(end);\nmeta(end).date = '2021-11-17';\nmeta(end).probe = 1;\n```\nShows that JGR2 used **Probe 1** on both days.\n\n---\n\n## 13. HELPER FUNCTIONS\n\n### getStructVarNames.m\n**File:** `/app/code/utils/getStructVarNames.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction varnames = getStructVarNames(obj)\n\nf = obj.bp;\nfnames = fieldnames(f);\nNtrials = f.Ntrials;\n\nvarnames = {};\n\nfor i = 1:numel(fnames)\n    if numel(f.(fnames{i}))==Ntrials && ~iscell(f.(fnames{i}))\n        varnames{end+1} = fnames{i};\n    else\n        thisvar = f.(fnames{i});\n        if isstruct(thisvar)\n            morefnames = fieldnames(thisvar);\n            for j = 1:numel(morefnames)\n                if numel(thisvar.(morefnames{j}))==Ntrials && ~iscell(thisvar.(morefnames{j}))\n                    varnames{end+1} = [fnames{i} '.' morefnames{j}];\n                end\n            end\n        end\n    end\nend\nend\n```\n\nExtracts all trial-by-trial variables from behavioral structure.\n\n### patternMatchCellArray.m\n**File:** `/app/code/utils/patternMatchCellArray.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [list,mask] = patternMatchCellArray(list,patterns,whichPatterns)\n\nif strcmpi(whichPatterns,'all')\n    fun = @(s)~cellfun('isempty',strfind(list,s));\n    out = cellfun(fun,patterns,'UniformOutput',false);\n    mask = all(horzcat(out{:}),2);\nelseif strcmpi(whichPatterns,'any')\n    mask = ismember(lower(list), lower(patterns));\nend\n\nlist = {list{mask}}';\nend\n```\n\nFinds cells in array matching patterns (case-insensitive).\n\n### findDataFn.m\n**File:** `/app/code/utils/findDataFn.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction fn = findDataFn(meta)\nobjpth = fullfile(meta.datapth,'DataObjects',meta.anm); \ncontents = dir(objpth);\ncontents = contents(~[contents.isdir]);\n\nfns = {contents.name}';\n\nfn = patternMatchCellArray({contents.name}',{'data_structure',meta.date},'all');\nif isempty(fn)\n    fn = patternMatchCellArray({contents.name}',{meta.datafn},'all');\nend\n\nif numel(fn) == 1\n    fn = fn{1};\nelseif numel(fn) == 2\n    ix = ~contains(fn,'._');\n    fn = fn{ix};\nelse\n    error(['Found multiple data_structures containing strings: ' meta.anm ' and/or ' meta.date ' in: ' objpth]);\nend\nend\n```\n\nLocates data file matching animal name and session date.\n\n### baselineFR.m\n**File:** `/app/code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m`\n\n```matlab\nfunction [presampleFR, presampleSigma] = baselineFR(obj,params,prbnum)\n\n% baselineFR - (nCells,nTrialTypes) mean FR for each cluster and trial type\n\nedges = -0.5:params.dt:mode(obj.bp.ev.sample);\ntime = edges + params.dt/2;\ntime = time(1:end-1);\n\n% get psths by condition\npsth = zeros(numel(time),numel(params.cluid{prbnum}),numel(params.condition));\nfor i = 1:numel(params.cluid{prbnum})\n    curClu = params.cluid{prbnum}(i);\n    for j = 1:numel(params.condition)\n        trix = params.trialid{j};\n        spkix = ismember(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trial, trix);\n\n        N = histc(obj.clu{prbnum}(curClu).trialtm(spkix), edges);\n        N = N(1:end-1);\n\n        psth(:,i,j) = mySmooth(N./numel(trix)./params.dt, params.smooth);\n    end\nend\n\npresampleFR = squeeze(median(psth,1));\npresampleSigma = squeeze(std(psth,[],1));\nend\n```\n\nComputes baseline firing rates during presample epoch (from stimulus start to go cue).\n\n---\n\n## 14. KEY PARAMETER SUMMARY\n\nFrom `getDefaultParams.m`:\n\n```matlab\n% Alignment and timing\nparams.alignEvent = 'goCue';           % alignment event for spike rasters\nparams.tmin = -2.5;                    % start time relative to align event (s)\nparams.tmax = 2.5;                     % end time relative to align event (s)\nparams.dt = 1/200;                     % bin size = 5 ms\nparams.lowFR = 0.5;                    % min FR threshold (spikes/s)\n\n% Smoothing\nparams.smooth = 15;                    % gaussian kernel window (15 bins = 75 ms)\nparams.bctype = 'none';                % boundary condition type\n\n% Trial conditions (logical expressions evaluated against obj.bp fields)\nparams.condition(1) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};\nparams.condition(2) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'};\nparams.condition(3) = {'R&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};\nparams.condition(4) = {'L&hit&~stim.enable&autowater&~early'};\n\n% Cluster quality\nparams.quality = {'all'};               % clusters to include (excludes: garbage, noisy, real?)\n\n% Probes and areas\nparams.probe(1) = 1;\nparams.probeArea{1} = 'ALM';\n\n% Video features (for each camera view)\nparams.traj_features = {\n    {'tongue','left_tongue','right_tongue','jaw','trident','nose'},\n    {'top_tongue','topleft_tongue','bottom_tongue','bottomleft_tongue','jaw','top_paw','bottom_paw','top_nostril','bottom_nostril'}\n};\n\n% Dimensionality reduction\nparams.feat_varToExplain = 80;          % PCA components explain 80% variance\n\n% Movement/video time offsets\nparams.prep = [-2.5 -0.05];             % prep epoch (s)\nparams.move = [-2.5 1.5];               % move epoch (s)\nparams.advance_movement = 0.0;          % video lead time (s)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 15. The AI hardcodes all session identifiers (animal, date, probe numbers, data directory) in two lists: `EPHYS_SESSIONS` and `RANDOMIZED_DELAY_SESSIONS`, derived from the reference loading scripts.\n\nii.\n```python\nALL_SESSIONS = EPHYS_SESSIONS + RANDOMIZED_DELAY_SESSIONS\n\ndef load_session_data(anm, date, data_dir):\n    data_path = os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, data_dir, f'data_structure_{anm}_{date}.mat')\n    try:\n        obj = mat73.loadmat(data_path)['obj']\n    except TypeError:\n        obj = _load_v5_session(data_path)\n    # Load motion energy\n    me_path = os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, data_dir, f'motionEnergy_{anm}_{date}.mat')\n    ...\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 2 that the data was organized in the two data directories, and in Step 1 that `loadObjs.m` and per-animal loading scripts were the reference for session selection. The AI explicitly excluded behavior-only sessions (MAH mice) and sessions not listed in loading scripts (JEB23_2023-10-20, JEB24_2023-10-03, JEB24_2023-10-04).\n\n## 1-b. How are the data split into subjects (mice)?\n\ni. Subjects are identified by the animal name string (e.g., 'EKH1', 'JEB7') from the hardcoded session registry. The AI collects unique animal names across all processed sessions and builds a sorted `subjects` list. A `subject_idx` array maps each session to its subject index. The AI identifies 14 unique subjects (10 from Ephys_Behavior, 4 from RandomizedDelay).\n\nii.\n```python\nsubjects_set = set()\nfor i, (anm, date, probes, data_dir) in enumerate(sessions):\n    ...\n    all_animals.append(anm)\n    subjects_set.add(anm)\nsubjects = sorted(subjects_set)\nsubject_idx = np.array([subjects.index(anm) for anm in all_animals], dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The AI noted in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 2 that there were 10 mice from Ephys_Behavior and 4 from RandomizedDelay. The AI acknowledged a discrepancy with the paper (which reports 9 DR mice) in Step 9, attributing it to \"minor discrepancy.\"\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. Each `.mat` file constitutes one session. The AI processes each session independently via the `process_session()` function, which loads the data, processes neural and behavioral variables, and returns per-trial arrays. Sessions with fewer than 2 valid trials or fewer than 10 neurons after filtering are skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor i, (anm, date, probes, data_dir) in enumerate(sessions):\n    result = process_session(anm, date, probes, data_dir, ...)\n    if result is None:\n        continue\n    all_neural.append(result['neural'])\n    ...\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 3 that the paper requires \"at least 10 units\" per session for inclusion.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Within each session, `obj.bp.Ntrials` gives the total number of trials. The AI creates boolean masks for trial properties (R, L, hit, miss, no, autowater, early, stim.enable) and selects valid trials based on filtering criteria. Each valid trial produces one entry in the neural, input, and output lists.\n\nii.\n```python\nntrials_total = int(bp['Ntrials'])\nR = np.array(bp['R']).flatten().astype(bool)\nL = np.array(bp['L']).flatten().astype(bool)\nhit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\nmiss = np.array(bp['miss']).flatten().astype(bool)\nno = np.array(bp['no']).flatten().astype(bool)\nautowater = np.array(bp['autowater']).flatten().astype(bool)\nearly = np.array(bp['early']).flatten().astype(bool)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5 that trials are indexed from 1 in MATLAB and that each trial has associated behavioral metadata.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies the following trial filters: exclude early lick trials (`early=1`), exclude stim trials (`stim.enable=1`), exclude no-response/ignore trials (`no=1`), and require a lick response (`hit | miss`). Notably, the AI keeps both hit (correct) and miss (incorrect) trials, unlike the reference code default conditions which only keep hit trials.\n\nii.\n```python\nvalid_mask = ~early & ~stim_enable & ~no\nvalid_mask = valid_mask & (hit | miss)\nvalid_trials = np.where(valid_mask)[0]\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Steps 3 and 5: \"Trial filtering: exclude early, stim.enable trials\" and \"Exclude early, stim.enable, and no-response trials. Keep hit and miss.\" The inclusion of miss trials is justified by the decoder task requiring an \"Outcome\" output (correct vs incorrect).\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `obj.clu`, which contains sorted spike clusters. For each cluster, the spike times are in `clu.trialtm` (time within trial) and `clu.trial` (trial assignment). The AI selects specific probe(s) per session based on the hardcoded session registry (matching the reference loading scripts' probe assignments for ALM).\n\nii.\n```python\nclu_data = obj['clu']\nfor probe_num in probes:\n    probe_idx = probe_num - 1\n    clu_probe = clu_data[probe_idx]\n    ...\n    trial_arr = np.array(clu_probe['trial'][clu_idx]).flatten()\n    trialtm_arr = np.array(clu_probe['trialtm'][clu_idx]).flatten()\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1 that `obj.clu` contains spike data with fields quality, site, tm, trial, trialtm, and spkWavs.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are aligned to the go cue by subtracting `goCue` time from each spike's `trialtm`. Aligned spikes are binned into 10 ms bins (dt=1/100) over a [-2.5, 2.5] s window using `np.histogram`. Bin counts are converted to firing rates (counts/dt) and smoothed with a causal Gaussian kernel (window=15 bins, bctype='reflect').\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 1.0 / 100  # 10 ms bins\nSMOOTH_WINDOW = 15\nBC_TYPE = 'reflect'\n\naligned = trialtm_arr[spk_mask] - align_times_all[j]\ncounts, _ = np.histogram(aligned, bins=edges)\nfr = counts.astype(np.float32) / DT\ntrialdat[:, neuron_offset + i, j] = causal_gaussian_smooth(fr, SMOOTH_WINDOW, BC_TYPE)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1 the pipeline: \"Align spikes to goCue: trialtm_aligned = trialtm - goCue_time. Bin spikes: histc(trialtm_aligned, edges). Smooth: mySmooth(N/dt, smooth, bctype).\"\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two-stage filtering: (1) Cluster quality filtering excludes clusters labeled 'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', or 'real?'. (2) Low firing rate filtering removes neurons with mean firing rate <= 1 Hz across all trials and time points.\n\nii.\n```python\nEXCLUDED_QUALITIES = {'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}\nLOW_FR = 1.0\n\ndef get_valid_cluster_indices(clu_probe, excluded_qualities=EXCLUDED_QUALITIES):\n    for i, q in enumerate(qualities):\n        if q_stripped.lower() not in {e.lower() for e in excluded_qualities}:\n            valid.append(i)\n\ndef remove_low_fr_neurons(trialdat, low_fr):\n    mean_fr = np.nanmean(np.nanmean(trialdat, axis=2), axis=0)\n    keep = mean_fr > low_fr\n    return trialdat[:, keep, :], keep\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 3: \"Neuron curation: Exclude quality labels: garbage, gabrga, noisy, real?. Then remove neurons with mean FR < 1 Hz.\" The AI cited the paper: \"firing rates exceeding 1 Hz.\"\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Each spike's trial-relative time (`trialtm`) has the go cue onset time (`goCue`) subtracted from it. The resulting aligned times are then binned into histogram bins centered on the go cue. The time axis is defined as `edges[:-1] + dt/2`, ranging from approximately -2.495 to 2.495 s.\n\nii.\n```python\nALIGN_EVENT = 'goCue'\ngoCue = np.array(ev['goCue']).flatten()\nalign_times_all = goCue\naligned = trialtm_arr[spk_mask] - align_times_all[j]\nedges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\ntime_axis = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\ncounts, _ = np.histogram(aligned, bins=edges)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1: \"alignEvent = 'goCue'\" and \"Align spikes to goCue: trialtm_aligned = trialtm - goCue_time.\"\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The temporal resolution is 10 ms (dt = 1/100 s), producing 500 time bins over the [-2.5, 2.5] s window. No rebinning is applied; spikes are directly binned at this resolution from raw spike times. The bin centers define the time axis.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 1.0 / 100  # 10 ms bins\nedges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\ntime_axis = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 4: \"Use 1/100 (10 ms) as in WorkingWithDataObjs.m\" and Step 5: \"Bin size 10 ms (dt=1/100): As in WorkingWithDataObjs.m, gives time axis from -2.5 to 2.5 s = 500 time bins.\"\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. The input is derived from the time axis computed from the binning parameters (TMIN, TMAX, DT), not directly from a raw data variable. It represents the center of each time bin relative to the go cue onset.\n\nii.\n```python\ninput_trials.append(time_axis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"Continuous time axis, ranges from -2.5 to 2.5 s.\"\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds*?\n\ni. The time axis is computed as bin centers: `edges[:-1] + dt/2`. This produces values from approximately -2.495 to 2.495 s in 0.01 s increments. The same time vector is used for every trial (since all trials have the same window).\n\nii.\n```python\nedges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\ntime_axis = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\ninput_trials.append(time_axis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. No specific justification given beyond it being a straightforward time axis computation.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input time axis is identical to the neural data time axis, since both use the same bin centers computed from the same edges. The neural data is binned using these edges, and the input is exactly these bin centers. Both are implicitly aligned to the go cue onset (t=0).\n\nii.\n```python\n# Same time_axis used for both:\ntime_axis = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n# Neural: binned using edges\ncounts, _ = np.histogram(aligned, bins=edges)\n# Input: the time axis itself\ninput_trials.append(time_axis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. No specific justification needed; alignment is inherent in the construction.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Lick direction is derived from four behavioral variables: `obj.bp.R` (right trial indicator), `obj.bp.L` (left trial indicator), `obj.bp.hit` (correct response), and `obj.bp.miss` (incorrect response).\n\nii.\n```python\nR = np.array(bp['R']).flatten().astype(bool)\nL = np.array(bp['L']).flatten().astype(bool)\nhit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\nmiss = np.array(bp['miss']).flatten().astype(bool)\nlick_right = (R & hit) | (L & miss)\nlick_direction = lick_right[valid_trials].astype(np.int32)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"R&hit or L&miss -> right(1); L&hit or R&miss -> left(0).\"\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction*?\n\ni. The AI determines actual lick direction (not instructed direction) by combining trial type (R/L) with outcome (hit/miss). On a hit (correct) trial, the mouse licked in the instructed direction. On a miss (incorrect) trial, the mouse licked in the opposite direction. Thus: right lick = (R&hit) | (L&miss), left lick = (L&hit) | (R&miss). The result is broadcast as a per-trial constant across all time bins (shape: (6, n_time) with lick_direction at index 0).\n\nii.\n```python\nlick_right = (R & hit) | (L & miss)\nlick_direction = lick_right[valid_trials].astype(np.int32)\nout[0, :] = lick_direction[t_idx]  # broadcast per-trial to time\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5 the mapping and noted it as per-trial output.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Behavioral context* derived from?\n\ni. Behavioral context is derived from `obj.bp.autowater`, a per-trial boolean flag indicating whether the trial was a water-cued (WC) trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nautowater = np.array(bp['autowater']).flatten().astype(bool)\ncontext = (~autowater[valid_trials]).astype(np.int32)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"autowater=1 -> WC(0); autowater=0 -> DR(1).\"\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Behavioral context*?\n\ni. Simple boolean inversion: autowater=True maps to WC=0, autowater=False maps to DR=1. Broadcast as per-trial constant across all time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\ncontext = (~autowater[valid_trials]).astype(np.int32)\nout[1, :] = context[t_idx]\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5 and noted that randomized delay sessions have context=DR for all trials.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Outcome is derived from `obj.bp.hit`, a per-trial boolean flag.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\noutcome = hit[valid_trials].astype(np.int32)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"hit -> correct(1); miss -> incorrect(0).\"\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. Direct mapping: hit=True maps to correct=1, hit=False (which is miss, given the trial filter requires hit|miss) maps to incorrect=0. Broadcast as per-trial constant across all time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\noutcome = hit[valid_trials].astype(np.int32)\nout[2, :] = outcome[t_idx]\n```\n\niii. Straightforward mapping documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue velocity* derived from?\n\ni. Tongue velocity is derived from `obj.traj[1]` (bottom camera view), specifically the `top_tongue` feature's x,y coordinates from the DeepLabCut tracking data (`traj.ts[:, 0:2, tongue_idx]`), along with `traj.frameTimes` for temporal alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\ntraj_bottom = obj['traj'][1]  # bottom cam\n# Find tongue feature index (top_tongue)\nfor i, name in enumerate(feat_names):\n    if name == 'top_tongue':\n        tongue_idx = i\n        break\nx = ts[:, 0, tongue_idx].copy()\ny = ts[:, 1, tongue_idx].copy()\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"Tongue velocity: Use tip-of-tongue displacement from bottom cam view, compute velocity as 1st derivative, take magnitude.\"\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. The AI computes tongue velocity at the native 400 Hz video frame rate: (1) extracts x,y position of `top_tongue` from bottom camera DLC data, (2) computes gradient (first derivative) at 400 Hz, scaled by VIDEO_FR to get pixels/second, (3) computes speed as sqrt(vx^2 + vy^2), (4) sets speed to NaN where tongue is not visible (NaN positions), (5) interpolates speed to neural time axis using `interp1d`, (6) sets remaining NaN values to 0 (tongue not visible = no movement).\n\nii.\n```python\nvx_all = np.gradient(x) * VIDEO_FR\nvy_all = np.gradient(y) * VIDEO_FR\nspeed = np.sqrt(vx_all**2 + vy_all**2)\nspeed[~valid] = np.nan\n# Interpolate to neural time axis\nf_interp = interp1d(old_time, speed, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\ntongue_vel[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n# Set NaN to 0\ntongue_vel = np.nan_to_num(tongue_vel, nan=0.0)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 6: \"Tongue NaN values NOT filled (per paper methods), velocity computed only where visible.\"\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Tongue velocity is discretized using per-session 50th percentile thresholding: values below the threshold are labeled 0 (\"low\"), values at or above are labeled 1 (\"high\"). If the threshold is 0 (many zeros from tongue-invisible periods), it is set to a tiny epsilon to ensure only exact zeros are \"low.\"\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_per_session(data_2d, percentile=50):\n    all_vals = data_2d[~np.isnan(data_2d)]\n    threshold = np.percentile(all_vals, percentile)\n    if threshold == 0:\n        threshold = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n    result = (data_2d >= threshold).astype(np.int32)\n    return result\n\ntongue_vel_disc = discretize_per_session(tongue_vel_valid)\n```\n\niii. The AI followed the decoder task instructions: \"discretized into two bins with per-session threshold: 0: < 50th percentile, 1: >= 50th percentile.\"\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Tongue velocity is aligned to the neural time axis by computing video frame times relative to the go cue, accounting for the video-neural offset (vidshift): `old_time = frameTimes - vidshift - alignTime`. The speed signal is then linearly interpolated from these aligned frame times to the neural time axis bin centers.\n\nii.\n```python\nft = np.array(traj_bottom['frameTimes'][trix]).flatten()\nold_time = ft - vidshift - align_times[trix]\nf_interp = interp1d(old_time, speed, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\ntongue_vel[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1 the video offset computation: \"vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart).\"\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Paw velocity* derived from?\n\ni. Paw velocity is derived from `obj.traj[1]` (bottom camera), using all features with 'paw' in the name (typically `top_paw` and `bottom_paw`), their x,y coordinates from DLC tracking, and `frameTimes` for alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\ntraj_bottom = obj['traj'][1]  # bottom cam\npaw_indices = []\nfor i, name in enumerate(feat_names):\n    if 'paw' in name.lower():\n        paw_indices.append(i)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"Paw velocity: Use paw position from bottom cam, compute velocity, take magnitude.\"\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Paw velocity*?\n\ni. For each paw feature: (1) extract x,y positions, (2) fill NaN values with nearest neighbor (unlike tongue), (3) compute gradient at 400 Hz scaled by VIDEO_FR, (4) compute speed as sqrt(vx^2+vy^2). Speeds from multiple paw features are averaged. The averaged speed is interpolated to the neural time axis. Remaining NaN values are filled with nearest neighbor.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor pidx in paw_indices:\n    x = ts[:, 0, pidx].copy()\n    y = ts[:, 1, pidx].copy()\n    # Fill NaN with nearest\n    for arr in [x, y]:\n        nans = np.isnan(arr)\n        if nans.any() and not nans.all():\n            ...\n            arr[nans] = arr[valid[nearest]]\n    vx = np.gradient(x) * VIDEO_FR\n    vy = np.gradient(y) * VIDEO_FR\n    speeds.append(np.sqrt(vx**2 + vy**2))\navg_speed = np.mean(speeds, axis=0)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 6: \"Paw NaN values filled with nearest neighbor before velocity computation.\"\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Paw velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Same as tongue velocity: per-session 50th percentile threshold. Values below → 0 (\"low\"), at or above → 1 (\"high\").\n\nii.\n```python\npaw_vel_disc = discretize_per_session(paw_vel_valid)\n```\n\niii. Follows decoder task instructions for 50th percentile discretization.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Paw velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same alignment method as tongue velocity: frame times adjusted by vidshift and go cue time, then interpolated to neural time axis.\n\nii.\n```python\nft = np.array(traj_bottom['frameTimes'][trix]).flatten()\nold_time = ft - vidshift - align_times[trix]\nf_interp = interp1d(old_time, avg_speed, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\npaw_vel[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n```\n\niii. Same justification as tongue velocity alignment.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Motion energy* derived from?\n\ni. Motion energy is loaded from separate `motionEnergy_ANM_DATE.mat` files. The raw data is in `me.data` (a cell array of per-trial motion energy time series at 400 Hz) and `me.moveThresh` (a per-session threshold). The `me.data` field may be nested inside a struct (`me.data.data`).\n\nii.\n```python\nme_path = os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, data_dir, f'motionEnergy_{anm}_{date}.mat')\nme_file = scipy.io.loadmat(me_path)\nme_var = me_file['me']\nif me_data.dtype.names and 'data' in me_data.dtype.names:\n    inner = me_data[0, 0]\n    me_raw = inner['data']\n    me_thresh = float(inner['moveThresh'].flat[0])\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1: \"Motion energy aligned: interp1(frameTimes - vidshift - alignTime, me.data, taxis).\"\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Motion energy*?\n\ni. Per-trial motion energy is interpolated from native 400 Hz video rate to the neural time axis. Video-neural offset is applied. NaN values at trial boundaries are filled with nearest neighbor values. The signal is then discretized.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_motion_energy_aligned(me_raw, obj, align_times, time_axis, vidshift, ntrials):\n    for trix in range(ntrials):\n        me_trial = np.array(me_raw[trix, 0]).flatten()\n        ft = np.array(traj_view0['frameTimes'][trix]).flatten()\n        old_time = ft - vidshift - align_times[trix]\n        f_interp = interp1d(old_time, me_trial, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n        me_aligned[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n    # Fill NaN with nearest\n    ...\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1 that this matches `loadMotionEnergy.m`: \"interp1(frameTimes - vidshift - alignTime, me.data, taxis)\" and \"fillmissing(,'nearest').\"\n\n## 9-c. How is `output` *Motion energy* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Motion energy is discretized using per-session 50th percentile thresholding, identical to tongue and paw velocity. The reference code instead uses `me.moveThresh` (a manually set per-session threshold separating bimodal distribution modes), but the AI follows the decoder task instructions which specify 50th percentile.\n\nii.\n```python\nme_disc = discretize_per_session(me_valid)\n```\n\niii. The AI followed the decoder task instructions: \"discretized into two bins with per-session threshold: 0: < 50th percentile, 1: >= 50th percentile.\"\n\n## 9-d. How is `output` *Motion energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Motion energy uses the side camera's (`obj.traj[0]`) `frameTimes` for alignment, with the same vidshift and go cue time offset. The signal is interpolated to the neural time axis.\n\nii.\n```python\ntraj_view0 = obj['traj'][0]  # side cam\nft = np.array(traj_view0['frameTimes'][trix]).flatten()\nold_time = ft - vidshift - align_times[trix]\nf_interp = interp1d(old_time, me_trial, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\nme_aligned[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n```\n\niii. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. The AI computes tongue velocity at the native 400 Hz video frame rate: (1) extracts x,y position of `top_tongue` from bottom camera DLC data, (2) computes gradient (first derivative) at 400 Hz, scaled by VIDEO_FR to get pixels/second, (3) computes speed as sqrt(vx^2 + vy^2), (4) sets speed to NaN where tongue is not visible (NaN positions), (5) interpolates speed to neural time axis using `interp1d`, (6) sets remaining NaN values to 0 (tongue not visible = no movement).\n\nii.\n```python\nvx_all = np.gradient(x) * VIDEO_FR\nvy_all = np.gradient(y) * VIDEO_FR\nspeed = np.sqrt(vx_all**2 + vy_all**2)\nspeed[~valid] = np.nan\n# Interpolate to neural time axis\nf_interp = interp1d(old_time, speed, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\ntongue_vel[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n# Set NaN to 0\ntongue_vel = np.nan_to_num(tongue_vel, nan=0.0)\n```\n\niii. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Tongue velocity is discretized using per-session 50th percentile thresholding: values below the threshold are labeled 0 (\"low\"), values at or above are labeled 1 (\"high\"). If the threshold is 0 (many zeros from tongue-invisible periods), it is set to a tiny epsilon to ensure only exact zeros are \"low.\"\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_per_session(data_2d, percentile=50):\n    all_vals = data_2d[~np.isnan(data_2d)]\n    threshold = np.percentile(all_vals, percentile)\n    if threshold == 0:\n        threshold = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n    result = (data_2d >= threshold).astype(np.int32)\n    return result\n\ntongue_vel_disc = discretize_per_session(tongue_vel_valid)\n```\n\niii. Tongue velocity is aligned to the neural time axis by computing video frame times relative to the go cue, accounting for the video-neural offset (vidshift): `old_time = frameTimes - vidshift - alignTime`.
